# 100 prompt đã điền sẵn, chỉ việc copy

Mỗi khối là một prompt hoàn chỉnh cho một con vật. Bấm copy ở góc khối rồi dán vào công cụ tạo ảnh, không cần sửa gì. Mọi con vật được vẽ trọn cả thân, trừ con thật khổng lồ (voi, hươu cao cổ, tê giác, hà mã, cá voi...) chỉ cần vẽ nửa thân trên, phần dưới khép kín gọn gàng. Không phần nào bị cắt ở bất kỳ cạnh nào. Pose và biểu cảm lấy theo ảnh mẫu (ló mặt, tay cầm đồ, đậu cành, nghiêng đầu, mắt to long lanh). Phần mảng đen trắng và nét gạch để tool tạo, nên ảnh sinh ra vẫn là ảnh màu phẳng. Riêng miệng há và mũi tối được yêu cầu tô đen tuyệt đối, lưỡi hồng nhạt một màu, để tool giữ đúng hình miệng thay vì trộn vào mảng bóng.


## Thú ăn thịt

### 1. Bengal tiger

```
Bengal tiger, full body in a low crouch, head turned three-quarter with a steady golden gaze, mouth closed, one front paw stepping forward, long tail curled behind.

FULL BODY, nothing cropped. Draw the WHOLE animal from head to toe: head, neck, torso, ALL legs or flippers or wings, feet or claws, and the tail, every part finished with its natural ending (paws, hooves, talons, fin tips, tail tip). The body is never cut, sliced or ended by a flat line: no part is cropped by ANY edge of the picture, and no part is left half-drawn. Choose the pose and camera angle so the entire animal fits a 2:3 portrait page (sitting, rearing, crouching, perched, coiled, leaping or curled poses fit best), standing or stretching diagonally if needed. The animal fills about 85% of the page height and as much width as the pose allows, with a clear white margin of about 5% on all four sides. The head is drawn large and slightly oversized (about one third of the animal's height) so the face stays big, clear and readable. EXCEPTION only for truly huge animals (elephant, giraffe, rhino, hippo, bison, moose, camel, whales, manta ray): the whole body is NOT required. Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. Never end the body with an oval, circular or sliced cut, and never let any part touch or cross an edge of the picture; keep a white margin on every side. Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. If a pose would push a part outside the frame, make the animal smaller or change the pose instead. The eyes are the focal point of the whole picture.

Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 60 to 120 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Not a photo, not a cartoon, not chibi.

Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: bright orange, amber, creamy white cheeks, black stripes, light green eyes, pink nose. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. Pure black is used only for outlines, pupils, nostrils, small markings, dark noses and the inside of an open mouth. Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

Aspect ratio 2:3, portrait.
```

FULL BODY, nothing cropped. The animal fills about 85% of the page height and as much width as the pose allows, with a clear white margin of about 5% on all four sides. The head is drawn large and slightly oversized (about one third of the animal's height) so the face stays big, clear and readable. EXCEPTION only for truly huge animals (elephant, giraffe, rhino, hippo, bison, moose, camel, whales, manta ray): the whole body is NOT required. Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. Never end the body with an oval, circular or sliced cut, and never let any part touch or cross an edge of the picture; keep a white margin on every side. Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. If a pose would push a part outside the frame, make the animal smaller or change the pose instead. The eyes are the focal point of the whole picture.

Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 60 to 120 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. Never end the body with an oval, circular or sliced cut, and never let any part touch or cross an edge of the picture; keep a white margin on every side. Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. If a pose would push a part outside the frame, make the animal smaller or change the pose instead. The eyes are the focal point of the whole picture.

Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 60 to 120 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Not a photo, not a cartoon, not chibi.

Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: grey-brown, tan bands, pink ears and nose. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. ### 41. Scarlet macaw

```
Scarlet macaw, wings folded with one foot raised as if waving, head tilted with a cheeky look, beak closed, one eye on the viewer.

FULL BODY, nothing cropped. Draw the WHOLE animal from head to toe: head, neck, torso, ALL legs or flippers or wings, feet or claws, and the tail, every part finished with its natural ending (paws, hooves, talons, fin tips, tail tip). The body is never cut, sliced or ended by a flat line: no part is cropped by ANY edge of the picture, and no part is left half-drawn. Choose the pose and camera angle so the entire animal fits a 2:3 portrait page (sitting, rearing, crouching, perched, coiled, leaping or curled poses fit best), standing or stretching diagonally if needed. The animal fills about 85% of the page height and as much width as the pose allows, with a clear white margin of about 5% on all four sides. The head is drawn large and slightly oversized (about one third of the animal's height) so the face stays big, clear and readable. EXCEPTION only for truly huge animals (elephant, giraffe, rhino, hippo, bison, moose, camel, whales, manta ray): the whole body is NOT required. Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. Never end the body with an oval, circular or sliced cut, and never let any part touch or cross an edge of the picture; keep a white margin on every side. Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. If a pose would push a part outside the frame, make the animal smaller or change the pose instead. The eyes are the focal point of the whole picture.

Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 60 to 120 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Not a photo, not a cartoon, not chibi.

Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: red, yellow, blue wing feathers, white face patch. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. Pure black is used only for outlines, pupils, nostrils, small markings, dark noses and the inside of an open mouth. Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

Aspect ratio 2:3, portrait.
```

A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). ```
West Indian manatee, head turned toward the viewer, whiskery snout in a big gentle smile, one paddle flipper waving, eye twinkling.

FULL BODY, nothing cropped. Draw the WHOLE animal from head to toe: head, neck, torso, ALL legs or flippers or wings, feet or claws, and the tail, every part finished with its natural ending (paws, hooves, talons, fin tips, tail tip). The body is never cut, sliced or ended by a flat line: no part is cropped by ANY edge of the picture, and no part is left half-drawn. Choose the pose and camera angle so the entire animal fits a 2:3 portrait page (sitting, rearing, crouching, perched, coiled, leaping or curled poses fit best), standing or stretching diagonally if needed. The animal fills about 85% of the page height and as much width as the pose allows, with a clear white margin of about 5% on all four sides. The head is drawn large and slightly oversized (about one third of the animal's height) so the face stays big, clear and readable. EXCEPTION only for truly huge animals (elephant, giraffe, rhino, hippo, bison, moose, camel, whales, manta ray): the whole body is NOT required. Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. Never end the body with an oval, circular or sliced cut, and never let any part touch or cross an edge of the picture; keep a white margin on every side. Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. If a pose would push a part outside the frame, make the animal smaller or change the pose instead. The eyes are the focal point of the whole picture.

Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 60 to 120 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Not a photo, not a cartoon, not chibi.

Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: grey, slate, pale belly, dark eye. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. Pure black is used only for outlines, pupils, nostrils, small markings, dark noses and the inside of an open mouth. Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

Aspect ratio 2:3, portrait.
```

Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: orange, cream spots, long thin legs, dark eyes. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. Pure black is used only for outlines, pupils, nostrils, small markings, dark noses and the inside of an open mouth. Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 60 to 120 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Not a photo, not a cartoon, not chibi.

Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: grey-brown, tan, dark scales, red tongue. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. Pure black is used only for outlines, pupils, nostrils, small markings, dark noses and the inside of an open mouth. Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

Aspect ratio 2:3, portrait.

The head is drawn large and slightly oversized (about one third of the animal's height) so the face stays big, clear and readable. EXCEPTION only for truly huge animals (elephant, giraffe, rhino, hippo, bison, moose, camel, whales, manta ray): the whole body is NOT required. Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. Never end the body with an oval, circular or sliced cut, and never let any part touch or cross an edge of the picture; keep a white margin on every side. Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. If a pose would push a part outside the frame, make the animal smaller or change the pose instead. The eyes are the focal point of the whole picture.

Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 60 to 120 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Not a photo, not a cartoon, not chibi.

Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: sand yellow, rust brown, spiky body, small dark eyes. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. Pure black is used only for outlines, pupils, nostrils, small markings, dark noses and the inside of an open mouth. Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

Aspect ratio 2:3, portrait.
```

### 93. European stag beetle

```
European stag beetle, standing tall with the large mandibles raised in a proud pose, front legs lifted.

FULL BODY, nothing cropped. Draw the WHOLE animal from head to toe: head, neck, torso, ALL legs or flippers or wings, feet or claws, and the tail, every part finished with its natural ending (paws, hooves, talons, fin tips, tail tip). Choose the pose and camera angle so the entire animal fits a 2:3 portrait page (sitting, rearing, crouching, perched, coiled, leaping or curled poses fit best), standing or stretching diagonally if needed. The animal fills about 85% of the page height and as much width as the pose allows, with a clear white margin of about 5% on all four sides. The head is drawn large and slightly oversized (about one third of the animal's height) so the face stays big, clear and readable. EXCEPTION only for truly huge animals (elephant, giraffe, rhino, hippo, bison, moose, camel, whales, manta ray): the whole body is NOT required. Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. Never end the body with an oval, circular or sliced cut, and never let any part touch or cross an edge of the picture; keep a white margin on every side. Every other animal, including all big cats, bears, gorilla, crocodile, shark and ostrich, MUST be drawn full body. If a pose would push a part outside the frame, make the animal smaller or change the pose instead. The eyes are the focal point of the whole picture.

Action and expression: this is the most important part. The animal is caught mid-action in a natural, dynamic pose with a strong diagonal line of action, never a stiff symmetrical portrait. The mood follows the pose and the animal, and is varied: calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, shy, grumpy, startled, joyful or fierce, and fierce or roaring only when it truly fits. Many poses keep the mouth closed, drawn as a simple thick line or a small smile. The face reads clearly from far away through clear shapes: eyes that match the mood, expressive brows, lifted or relaxed ears, a head tilt. When the mouth is open it is one large clear shape.

Style: charming, characterful animal illustration in a bold geometric faceted vector style (modern low-poly poster), FULLY COLORED. The animal has personality and a lively, storybook-poster feel: natural, species-accurate anatomy, slightly appealing proportions, and a clear, characterful expression that suits the pose (calm, proud, curious, sly, sleepy, playful, startled, gleeful or fierce). The eyes are large and glossy, with a clear iris, a solid black pupil and one or two big white highlights, and their shape, lids and brows express the emotion. The body is built from about 40 to 80 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Not a photo, not a cartoon, not chibi.

Color and light: flat cel-shading with hard-edged light from the upper left (left side lighter, right side in shadow). Natural colors of the species: glossy brown-black, chestnut mandibles, dark legs. Strong contrast between tonal steps, but NO large black areas: the deepest shadow tone is a dark but clearly visible color (dark grey, dark brown, dark green), never pure black. Pure black is used only for outlines, pupils, nostrils, small markings, dark noses and the inside of an open mouth. Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

An open mouth uses exactly three flat colors and nothing else: the mouth and throat are ONE solid pure-black shape (no red, maroon or brown inside the mouth, no shading), the tongue is ONE flat light pink shape (clearly lighter than the fur around the mouth), and the teeth are plain white; each part has a thick black outline. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous. Every shape and every black area is large and intentional, no smaller than 3% of the image width. Whiskers, antennae, legs and other thin parts are drawn as a few clean thick lines or simplified into solid shapes.

Background: pure flat white (#FFFFFF), nothing else. Only the animal, plus the single simple prop named in the pose if any (a branch, a bamboo shoot, a leaf, a stem), drawn as large solid shapes. No scenery, no ground, no water, no sky, no shadow, no frame, no border. No text, watermark, signature, logo or symbols.

Aspect ratio 2:3, portrait.
```

### 99. Leafcutter ant

```
Leafcutter ant, front view, mandibles clenched, determined eyes, carrying a large green leaf piece over its head.

FULL BODY, nothing cropped. Draw the WHOLE animal from head to toe: head, neck, torso, ALL legs or flippers or wings, feet or claws, and the tail, every part finished with its natural ending (paws, hooves, talons, fin tips, tail tip). Choose the pose and camera angle so the entire animal fits a 2:3 portrait page (sitting, rearing, crouching, perched, coiled, leaping or curled poses fit best), standing or stretching diagonally if needed. The animal fills about 85% of the page height and as much width as the pose allows, with a clear white margin of about 5% on all four sides. The head is drawn large and slightly oversized (about one third of the animal's height) so the face stays big, clear and readable. EXCEPTION only for truly huge animals (elephant, giraffe, rhino, hippo, bison, moose, camel, whales, manta ray): the whole body is NOT required. Show the head, neck, chest, shoulders and both front legs or flippers with the feet or fin tips fully drawn, and let the body end naturally at the chest or belly line with a clean thick outline, as if the rest is simply left out. The body is built from about 40 to 80 LARGE flat facets (triangles and irregular polygons) that follow the form of the animal. Every facet is ONE solid flat color with crisp straight edges, and neighboring facets differ clearly in brightness (light, medium, dark steps). Thick, smooth, solid pure-black outlines: heaviest on the outer silhouette, medium on the main facet edges. The edge of fur, feathers or scales has a few large sharp pointed shapes, not many small spikes. Dark markings are bold, solid black shapes with tapered tips, placed deliberately and not too numerous; each single marking is small to medium (no wider than about 8% of the image width). Shadows under the head and on the neck are drawn as dark colored facets, not as solid black shapes. A dark nose is solid pure black, not dark brown. Small features (nose, mouth, inner ear, eyes) use at most two flat colors each, and two touching colors must never be close in brightness. No gradients, no soft shading, no painterly texture.

Keep it clean: no hatching, no cross-hatching, no dots, no speckles, no stippling, no scratches, no sketchy strokes, no fine detail, no noise. Every line is smooth and continuous.
